## Ordinary completion and inline-chain cancellation arbitration

Ordinary completion captures its actual lifecycle owner, then checks the same
nonterminal/noncancelled owner and writes COMPLETED inside one Database-owned
BEGIN IMMEDIATE. Cancellation, missing/terminal rows and replaced agent/SID,
claim or delegated state return False without success verdict/history/parent
or barrier effects. Heartbeat/updated clocks do not replace lifecycle ownership.
Sessionless carriers remain valid; accepted recovery keeps the separate exact
ledger/INTEGER-result conditional writer. Root before/after captures remain
outside the native teardown/publication tail, with both unwind layers and whole
failed-final-batch suppression intact.

Inline-chain advance and completed-trigger clear writers compare the original
noncancelled in_progress/delegated parent, exact chain, newest triggering child
and selected raw INTEGER report after the SAME BEGIN IMMEDIATE as chain/child/
attachment-link writes. They never refresh into a replacement owner. Modern
agent/SID selection fails closed on absent, malformed or nonterminal reports;
a genuine report-landed/in_progress child remains eligible, including its later
completion under the same report. Only genuine legacy fingerprints retain the
original task-wide fallback. Unresolved-failure clears retain their separate
failed-lineage/sibling checks and conditionally compare the original parent/chain.
Configured reviewer and expected-verdict gates stay.

The caller receives explicit advance, chain_complete, aborted or lost_owner.
Only authenticated chain_complete may complete the sessionless carrier, and the
helper returns the actual completion Boolean before feeding its enclosing barrier.
Attachment failure, an insertion integrity error with successful rollback, or
unverifiable evidence aborts through the existing carrier-failure/ordinary-owner
wake path with its causal note. A stale selection produces no wake or success.
Operational/native/COMMIT/rollback uncertainty raises with the original cause and
actual secondaries. Audit/queue publication follows successful COMMIT; later
publication refusal retains that exact committed child/prefix without replay.
Native teardown and parent publication remain outside writer/capture locks.
The historical cancel-race note-deferral sketch is superseded by these current
maintained boundaries. The CURRENT indexed inline-chain spec retains historical
sketches; its former cancel-clears passage is superseded by this arbitration
contract and its reconciled cancellation invariant. Cancellation itself is unchanged.

C1 binds one org-owned coordinator to task preparation and final launch, retaining

Installed-layout unowned legacy producers refuse before claim/preparation. Producer binding/root gate → move

Provider return never proves containment. Durable Pause attribution remains through
workflow reconciliation and the actual outer cleanup batch. Its explicit disposition

Retry attribution uses the existing entry id, task/agent/SID, owner identity,
fingerprint, generation and retry fields; the retained reservation adopts that
entry id only after journal COMMIT. The closed Pause journal format is unchanged.
Failed-tail diagnostics append to existing `cleanup.suffix_lines` within its
65536-byte UTF8 allowance, preserving earlier lines; excess new diagnostics are
explicitly truncated and logged. `execution_unknown` retains ownership independently
of diagnostic capacity. These lines are diagnostics, never quiescence proof or

and propagates unless an original exception is already unwinding; then that
original exception carries the settlement failure note and log. Every collected

## Current name context in task prompts

TASK10385 binds a presentation-only current metadata reader from OrgState to
Orchestrator. The task role builder renders Name · ID for members selected by
existing `_list_candidate_agents` IDs; it never substitutes labels into delegation,
chains, fanout, reviewer rules or callback examples. The outer task wrapper
refreshes self/founder context on every construction, including leaf/self-only
and resumed work. Its outer agent, task/session/provider IDs remain canonical.

Task role and outer wrapper are separate bounded metadata reads. All labels in
each rendering share that read; no file/DB lookup per label or persisted snapshot
is added. Founder is separate human context, never a roster agent. Missing naming
metadata produces honest ID-only context with no writes, reconciliation or
launch gate. Prompt wording states current-name human addressing and ID-only
automation; remembered names do not override fresh resumed-turn context.

Full/resumed thread, full fallback and resumed nudge plus dream/wake/schedule
production callers supply fresh context under the same rule. IDs retain authority,
role checks, durable queue membership and callback/failure ordering. Scenario8
renders shipping builders and scenario9 checks bindings. Source-pinned maker
E2E receipts do not establish real provider-resume launches or independent
acceptance. UI/type source is published; final independent whole-candidate
review/QA/checks and guarded merge remain required.
See the [current naming contract](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-identity-names-core.md).

## Naming v1 core, API and routing source

Naming install alone uses N-SAC-1 transaction-time structural admission over the
selected org DB, actual task/audit dependencies, closed naming interactions and
unchanged complete workflow/Pause/move validators. Global workflow/legacy
references retain their strict contract; naming readiness grants no authority.
Reads and ordinary non-install refreshes never invoke admission or install names.

Org identity names use the same outer coroutine gate as supported roster and
consumer writes, with profile-before-publication ordering preserved. The thread
RLock is distinct and cannot serialize yielding coroutine writers. Naming helpers
capture twice outside short leases and project after real terminal compensation;
pure rename never calls canonical_change. IDs continue to own session bindings,
automated decisions, team membership and canonical definition filenames.

The bounded API delegates operator rename to that same CAS/gate/audit service;
it adds no canonical generation/publication, receipt or authority/profile
algorithm. Raw task/session/agent/composer/speaker/thread/invocation binding-key
presence (including null/partial/malformed) is denied before strict body decoding.
Read resolution preserves lifecycle and current team/thread context predicates
and returns typed founder-human/former diagnostics; it is never an authorization
or reservation. Subsequent action owners revalidate canonical eligibility. No
label becomes a session principal, automated target, team participant or executor.
Current and resumed prompt rosters are implemented by the presentation-only
reader described above.


Current routing source resolves names/IDs case-insensitively with the unchanged
@token grammar. Former inputs refuse409 with current_name before thread creation,
stale closure, callback settlement or application attachment finalization. A
lookup grants no reservation; action boundaries revalidate. Actors/proofs remain
ID-only. Current-founder-only messages suppress new broadcast/cohort wakes, retain
full-recipient obligations and enqueue prior catch-up separately. Founder remains
human inbox only. Restart uses durable IDs/ranges/tokens/exchange/deferral rows;
no historical body reclassification or schema addition occurs. Filesystem capture
precedes the RLock and multipart reads precede final no-await admission/write.
Unavailable naming keeps actual ID behavior; non-ID addresses refuse503.

**H handoff storage — U3a(a), THR139 seq445/465.** Proven-fresh org creation

columns, six automatic constraint indexes, no marker or explicit index).

`python scripts/migrate_workflow_author_handoff_schema.py --org-root <absolute-org-root> --org-slug <slug> [--check]`.
For this H command, `--check` returns 1 for valid G requiring migration, 0 for
validated H; ordinary migration/repeat returns 0, refusal 1, parser error 2.
One short writer rechecks source ownership and complete independent references,
adds only the approved table, validates the target and retained rows, then
commits; failures roll back. Operator stop/ownership cooperation is required;
the script stops no process and cannot exclude an arbitrary concurrent start.
Even empty H requires an H-compatible reader. F/E/G SQL, IDs, history, permanent
NULL active legacy result and genuine INTEGER result links retain their meaning.
Whole-database F/E/G/H references preserve the independent Pause/Resume dimension;
generic Database/runtime-audit remain workflow-free and authority-v2 observed-only.

Complete H validation authenticates historical profile readiness from the saved
full dependency/store/registry mirror. Every closed variant preserves raw
nonempty source profile names; namespace and consumer identities are nonblank
UTF8 strings of 1..256 bytes without NUL, generations are exact integers in
0..9223372036854775807, and digests are full lowercase SHA256 strings. Native
row grouping/order and unrelated or removed rows remain intact. The historical
org's active requirements must have matching active store and registry
generations, and their full names, digests and ordered consumer membership must
reconstruct the pinned canonical profile projection. Empty mirror with required
profiles, missing/mismatched required rows or unbound requirements refuse
`workflow_author_handoff_storage_corrupt` before graph creation; an empty
required projection remains valid. Readiness, the offline script and producers
share this reader. Admitted GET exposes only category500; invisible scope404 and
selected-grant403 retain earlier precedence. Today's mutable mirror is never
historical evidence; a genuine later authority change remains a current hold.

New H structured witnesses compare canonical JSON bytes against authentic
retained or derived projections, including the row, immutable source intent,
activation/template and byte pins, context, snapshot, saved pointer and profile
closure. Boolean true/false cannot substitute for integer 1/0 in any such
witness. Exact integers and legitimate embedded snapshot booleans retain their
types; historical event numeric semantics remain unchanged. A mismatch refuses
`workflow_author_handoff_storage_corrupt` before graph effects, with the same
selected scope/grant precedence.

New F5 review-dispatch events also preserve types across every before/after
projection, immutable transition, adjacent event and final retained row. Each
intermediate projection anchors immutable fields to native rows and validates
mutable field types; the initial projection derives the producer's queued
values. Complete callback records and reused results retain native task_results
types, acceptance is exact integer 1, and host quiescence is genuine Boolean
true. Boolean/integer substitutions refuse before owned effects, using
`workflow_review_storage_corrupt` internally and the existing admitted H
category500 mapping. Authentic flags and historical S2 numeric semantics remain.

U3a(b) adds matched immutable `@3` template/activation/context readers,
`submissions.validate_handoff_graph` and the internal handoff/review producers.
Historical `@1`/`@2` families and pins retain their interpretation. An original
CURRENT author with stage+submit authority reserves immutable bytes only. SAME
operation fulfillment requires its genuine INTEGER/full callback and committed
successful/quiescent terminal proof, plus current authority and independence.
The atomic graph retains NULL legacy result, appends the authentic result link,
and creates one unsigned request per configured slot. Actual agent tasks alone
populate the ordered 0..3 reviewer-task IDs; roles, kinds, outcomes and timing
come from immutable template data. Inputs remain filtered to each recipient.

The review owner reuses the existing scheduler/native host lifecycle and
Pause/Resume final admission, publication, prompt reader and cancellation fences.
Completed-source discovery precedes workers and periodic enqueue hints; possible
launch without affirmative proof remains uncertain. No callback is a signature.
U3a(c) mounts the closed handoff POST/GET and unsigned selected-request GET,
with CLI/TypeScript/OpenAPI/native parity and existing F5 callback, replay,
cancellation and portability owner selection. This complete U3a composition remains
unaccepted pending renewed independent same-head assessment and current static gates.
Direct-submit remains deferred; reviewer staging/native denial are described above. Genuine
receipts/joins are the dependency-incomplete U3b checkpoint; revisions/reassignment/
recovery, mounted operator UI and operator acceptance remain later units. Full independent review, honest same-head QA,
unconditional exact-head Source Boundary Lint and Web Lint (Node 24), and
manager guarded merge remain required under THR291 seq69. Actual CLI callback
sources and isolation fences remain retained; execution is not a merge check.
Manager-owned bounded two-template public smoke must precede Founder invitation.
No live migration/enable/assignment/deploy/restart follows.

**G submission-schema migration (THR139 seq395; retained F/E→G path).**
The earlier fresh initializer selected G; U3a(a) now selects H under the H contract.
Existing F/E startup,
reopen and enable retain their installed layout; S2 remains available on E.
The explicit org-only operator command is
`python scripts/migrate_workflow_submission_schema.py --runtime-root <absolute-root> --org <slug> [--check]`.
Check returns migration-needed (3) for valid F/E, ready (0) for complete G,
refused (1) for invalid source/ownership, and parser errors return 2. Actual
migration atomically replaces only submissions/events and adds the approved
three tables/two explicit indexes. Event revisions come from an unambiguous
retained submission/round/event/replay closure; ambiguity refuses without
rewriting history. Every G database, including an empty one, needs a compatible
reader. The original F/E definitions and pristine-F downgrade contract remain.
The older draft script still upgrades F to E and reports a validated G as a
no-write G replay. Legacy authority comparison uses independent complete
F/E/G whole-database references; authority-v2 remains observed-only.

An authorized upgrade requires the daemon to be stopped with its configured
home/registration observable and source owners/hosts reconciled. The command
reads bounded existing PID/port/registry evidence and reserves one SQLite
writer; it stops no process and provides no exclusion against an arbitrary
concurrent daemon start. Operator cooperation is a precondition. Active-origin
submissions retain NULL legacy result identity; separate authenticated operation
and INTEGER ordinary-result links preserve the actual result evidence.
U3a(b) internal submission/review/link producers are staged in the stacked draft.
Genuine receipts/joins/finalization, later U3-U6 units and
independent operator acceptance, including real Founder UI actions, remain pending. This implementation work does not authorize live migration,
enablement, deployment or a Phase1-completion claim.


## Founder-managed Default (THR296)


Task-completion prompt wording follows the task owner: a worker-owned root may
finish, self-delegate or escalate within its server-authorized contract. An
attempted non-root founder escalation fails the child and wakes its parent.
Manager-only operations retain their restrictions; the canonical start-task skill owns the
payload guidance. Delegated leaves still omit decisions and callback identity
and final-action ordering remain unchanged.

Offline roster recovery uses the retained native operation/journal/request
identity. Replay performs no reset/materializer/publication when actual durable
completion is already established. Explicit compensation publishes a new
coherent original-roster generation, retaining cleared resumes and audits; the
original successful receipt is preserved. C6/C8/C9 observers/localizations are
authored source, with M/guest-reboot behavior unexecuted.

Attachment and executable authority are distinct. An exactly registered pending
bootstrap manager can attach without a current manager grant; owner admission and
workflow capture still require its ordinary supported promotion to ACTIVE. Active/
pending duplicate identities or mismatched pending manager role/team refuse.

Founder-managed teams use a human principal without an executable manager.
Explicit active worker owners may run ordinary roots and self-decompose; they
have no peer/fanout, manager administration, policy or template authority.
Missing owners for a human team are refused before persistence. Malformed active
definitions after attachment produce the existing unknown_owner admission refusal
before task/attachment allocation. Delegated
outcomes use a valid persisted executable parent owner, with unknown_manager
for invalid ancestry. Null verdict uses the native implicit mapping; blank and
custom strings remain exact. This audit is an agent outcome, never human approval.
Selected human failed-leaf recovery is result-scoped and conservatively refuses
foreign/multiple/unreadable history. Separate evidence, chain, fanout, FAILED,
review and marker commits remain separate; history and review precede marker.
Retained loop retries wait 50ms outside locks without a retry cap. Busy/pending
is not settlement; parent handoff follows genuine owned cleanup. A native drain
exception or cancellation retains the exceptional operation, phase and captured
jobs. Same-process same-result reentry returns `recovery_required` without new
settlement, parent handoff or success-only bookkeeping; FAILED job rows do not
prove cleanup. Pending callers join the original owner. Shutdown preserves
accepted/consumed residue for existing next-start recovery. Healthy finished
same-result reentry checks current ownership/history again; cancellation or a
replacement binding cannot receive a cached done disposition.

## Conventions

- Type hints on all function signatures.
- `from __future__ import annotations` in every source file.
- Pydantic v2 for structured data.
- `StrEnum` for enumerations.
- Agent names are plain strings; agents are discovered dynamically from `<runtime>/orgs/<slug>/org/agents/*.md`.
- Tests should cover business logic such as escalation rules and audit-log shape.

`README.md` is for end users. `CLAUDE.md` is for repo-wide agent instructions. Implementation and behavior tests own runtime rules. These guides explain them; `docs/superpowers/specs/` retains design history.

When starting a feature, read the relevant design doc first and follow existing patterns in `runtime/orchestrator/`.


## Independent nightly disk sweep (THR307)

One daemon-wide operator control is `<daemon_home>/disk-sweep/stop.json`.
Its only supported content is a JSON object containing exactly `stop` with a
real Boolean: `{"stop":true}` suspends scheduled deletion for all orgs;
`{"stop":false}` or a genuinely absent literal file permits the default policy.
The control is read afresh once per regular scheduled attempt, after the normal
same-night reservation and before native writer reservation or worker launch,
inside the shared1800s budget. Invalid/unreadable/oversized/truncated/changed
content, wrong/extra/duplicate keys or types, invalid UTF8, symlinks (including
dangling links or aliased parents), hardlinks, directories and special files
visibly suspend. Only final-file ENOENT means absent; other I/O failures suspend.
Malformed control does not prevent daemon/org startup. Operators alone write or
remove this local file; there is no per-org override, setting/API or new schedule.
Suspension logs its cause and, when publication is safe, writes `outcome:
suspended` with daemon-scoped `scheduled_admission` (`path,state,reason` and an
error when invalid) in the existing summary/audit payload. All orgs remain
unreached, with no worker/PID, reaping, removal-byte or successful-sweep claim.
The existing audit empty-task/daemon envelope and org ordering remain. Marker
and report reads/writes traverse literal no-follow parents; publication uses a
new exclusive pending inode and descriptor-relative replacement. A missing or
aliased parent, aliased/hardlinked/special final metadata or existing pending
entry refuses without writing through that path. Scheduled work never creates
a missing control parent. Refusal is visible through the existing daemon log;
a summary or org audit may be unavailable and must not be claimed as persisted.
Only manual preview can create missing report parents, with no-follow traversal.
After the single stop observation, the actual initial date summary is published
before any native writer reservation. Allowed admission initially records
`incomplete`, empty org results and all orgs unreached, without a finished/PID or
mutation claim. Unsafe date-summary or pending metadata therefore refuses with
zero reservations or worker effects. Later checkpoint/publication failures after
genuine reservation retain native uncertainty; this initial observation is not
a fence against subsequent filesystem changes.
These are filesystem observations, not a racing-rename fence or hard real-time
certification. Publication failure never permits deletion. Manual `--dry-run` remains usable and nonmutating with
true, invalid or absent control; it may write its existing reports/audits.
Removing/fixing the file affects the NEXT existing scheduled attempt only:
no same-night catchup/retry or marker reset. This is a run-admission boundary;
writing stop does not cancel an already launched worker or promise a hard
real-time stop. Existing deletion/ownership/age/containment gates still apply.

Founder THR307seq134/136 makes scheduled deletion effective for EVERY normally
admitted org, including missing config, legacy off/dry_run/on and newly admitted
orgs. The obsolete `disk_sweep` key is ignored regardless of shape/value; there is
no per-org opt-out or replacement setting. Unrelated config validation remains.
Reports carry actual `mode` (scheduled on, explicit preview dry_run), config digest
and no `configured_mode`. Explicit manual preview inventories even legacy off.
This is independent of `workspace_cleanup` and its unchanged legacy settings.
Machine-local03:00 (not org timezone), next future occurrence only, sequential org order and one1800s monotonic
batch budget. No cursor/rotation or candidate count cap. Unknown eligibility and
unreached scope retain; the owned worker process group is stopped on deadline or
shutdown. `output/` and durable scratch manifest/lock siblings stay excluded.

Actual task AND structural root must each be completed/failed/cancelled/superseded.
Recognized finished jobs completed/failed/rejected are skipped before owner-lineage
lookup; active jobs still require valid lineage and retain their structural root.
Unknown job status/active lineage retains. Recursive
mtime+ctime age of at least one hour and no-follow/same-filesystem containment
apply to both phases. Git additionally requires owned registration,
clean/nonignored-untracked status and HEAD reachability through retained local
branches/remotes; branches remain, ignored contents are disposable. Git removal
is nonforce, with at most one fully refreshed contained repair/retry. Dry run
never chmods/removes/prunes. Before trusting Git exit0, a complete no-follow
linked-admin census reconciles entries, metadata pointers and worktree backlinks
with Git output and literal task roots. Missing/unreadable/aliased/conflicting
metadata retains that entire repository as a literal unknown scope with null
candidate count; independent scopes continue within the same deadline. Identities
are refreshed before Git removal, repair, retry and prune; earlier partial effects
stay unknown rather than becoming zero-effect success. A genuinely absent admin
with primary-only inventory and no task roots remains valid. Partial discovery
and uncertain Git-prune scope prohibit prune.

Manual dry run: `python -m runtime.daemon.disk_sweeper --dry-run --runtime-root
/absolute/runtime` (one command). JSON is in `<daemon_home>/disk-sweep/`; existing
org audit action is `disk_sweep_summary`. Allocation is inode-deduped and successful
removal-attributed; retained/partial/unreached bytes and physical freed space stay
unknown, whole-disk percent is context only. Below20% is flagged in audit/JSON;
no new notification interface. Founder THR307 seq117 removes all OS-process
eligibility inspection from this nightly sweeper, including process namespace,
cwd/root/exe/fd/environment and the former four-name/zombie policy. Top-level
JSON and org/audit `process_scope` state that process usage is not checked;
`process_exemptions` remains an unused empty compatibility field. This claims
neither process absence nor executable authentication. Filesystem mount boundaries
still retain via `/proc/self/mountinfo`; other owners' legacy/root-terminal process
gates are unchanged. Ended tasks/root status does not prove OS-process quiescence.


See the [full current contract](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-nightly-disk-sweeper.md)
for evidence semantics, limitations, finite disposable verification and staged
operational acceptance. Root TASK12353 owns reviewed delivery and normal rollout,
serving verification and seven actual scheduled on-night receipts. Founder
seq134/136 already authorizes all-org activation after delivery; no additional
activation decision is required. Source publication alone does not change loaded
code. Original TASK11742/JOB4898 recovery and root11948/PR1128 retain their separate
ownership/evidence. Seven legacy agent sessions remain until seven actual approved
on-night observations and a separately approved cutover; no manual cleanup fallback.

## Workspace Cleanup Configuration

`OrgConfig` parses `workspace_cleanup.enabled` as the existing scheduler switch:
it is boolean and defaults to `true`. The enabled scheduler evaluates the daily
local 03:30 occurrence in the org timezone (existing occurrences delimit half-open
windows compared as UTC instants; a nonexistent spring-forward wall time is skipped
and an ambiguous fall-back wall time takes the first `fold=0` instance). It suppresses
when any marker exists at/after the current boundary (including legacy weekly rows) or
any prior marker is unfinished, and it applies no rolling cooldown. Its separate
`workspace_cleanup.reclamation_actions_enabled` key is also strictly boolean,
defaults to `false`, and gates the bounded pre-agent reclamation hook. A true
value does not bypass the hook's registered-owner guard, fresh owner, provenance,
deadline, or consumer admissions. The hook acts only on a third-or-later cleanup
ordinal whose scheduler-created preclaim owner is assigned to a registered
in-memory `TeamsRegistry` agent and reconciles to the invocation's initial
successful `0 -> 1` claim (the first two runs stay report-only); disabling the
key affects later admissions only and cannot revoke an already admitted call.

Routine scheduled and exact-marker manual cleanup reports remain durable on the
existing agent page via ordinary completion/results (THR-259 seq418). The scheduler
admits a clean-root task without report-thread configuration, lookup, ID allocation
or composite creation. Task ID allocation, composition and ordinary insertion stay
synchronous under `org.db_lock` after awaited measurement; enqueue follows successful
insertion. Complete marker history, ordinals, cadence and deduplication are unchanged.
There is no routine thread posting/reuse obligation. Preserve all historical threads,
messages, associations, results and audits, explicit founder-requested coordination,
truthful anomalies/partial failures/unknown bytes/independent verification gaps and
the current-session final `happyranch report-completion` callback. Exact manual
first-line display eligibility (alone/LF/CRLF) neither creates trigger audits nor
changes daemon count or action authority. Runtime and canonical bundled-skill
rollout require separately authorized deployment after merge.

Cleanup activity uses three nonunique indexes installed with `IF NOT EXISTS`
after legacy columns exist: tasks(assigned_agent,created_at DESC,id DESC),
audit_log(task_id,agent) where action='workspace_cleanup_triggered', and
task_results(task_id,agent,id DESC). Existing definitions are not validated;
reader SQL, history and cleanup authority are unchanged. Breaker listing/mint
use sequential `await asyncio.to_thread` calls and the original DB RLock, also
used by close. Removal can log a sweep error; cancellation can leave a worker
running and a committed pending token for later attached-tick recovery. No
org lifetime lock, worker drain or new shutdown guarantee is added.

The daily trigger and manual dispatch share the ONE `workspace-cleanup` TASK
system contract (`requires_repo=false`; source
`runtime/skills/bundled/workspace-cleanup/SKILL.md`). Manual dispatch requires
the exact first line `HAPPYRANCH SYSTEM WORKSPACE CLEANUP RUN (manual-dispatch)`;
an unmarked manual request is inventory-only. The bundled read-only
`scripts/check_path_use.py` returns only `clear_observation`, `blocked`, or
`unknown`: an authoritative recorded terminal status plus a fresh complete
same-user process scan replaces separate live-session/task-to-process identity
(THR-259 seq171), and a fixed login/session daemon qualifies only by exact
readable process name AND exact bounded cgroup role (THR-259 seq185) and is
deliberately uninspected. Any other unreadable same-user process is `unknown`
and skips; root is outside the scan; positive non-exempt use blocks. This is a
snapshot with disclosed later-opener/data-loss residual, not proof of OS-wide
absence.

The current-use scanner treats a parseable leader `Z` as evidence for that task
only. It independently enumerates the bounded group frontier before requiring
leader namespaces or references. Repeated PID/TID, Tgid, starttime and complete
UID identity with repeated `Z` may omit only that task's dead references; live
workers still require their own `cwd`, `root`, `exe`, `maps`, private fd table,
mount/user namespaces and path resolution through their own proc root. Missing
worker census after leader exit remains `unknown`; `Threads: 1`, a controller's
TIDs or a guessed singleton never substitute for a complete census. Canonical
positive ASCII TIDs, uniqueness, actual group membership and unique complete
Pid/Tgid/Uid/State status fields are required. Malformed identities, reuse or
credential changes refuse; ordinary live R/S transitions alone are not reuse.
Thread membership/count observations converge within the existing pass cap
and shared deadline, reserving final census and identity/view checks. Known
positive use remains `blocked` even with incomplete evidence. Public result,
coverage and row schemas, scan/Bounds compatibility and exits 0/3/2 are retained.

For receipts from this revised source, `scanned` counts distinct admitted
non-exempt group leaders; `exited` counts distinct confirmed terminal task
identities (Tgid,TID,starttime,all four UIDs), with initially absent unbracketed
PID census slots accounted separately; `vanished` counts deduplicated actual
absence/closed-reference observations, including separately observed reopened
reference incarnations. A present confirmed Z contributes one exited and zero
vanished; leader/task-leader aliases and rediscovery do not recount it. These
counters are different units and must not be summed as population proof.
Historical receipts retain their original source-bound event meanings and
bytes. This correction grants no cleanup, package refresh, deployment or
live-process unreadability waiver.

Preparation and outer-batch evidence use the packaged `prepare_cleanup.py` and
`observe_cleanup_batch.py` (THR-259 seq506). Compare explicit aware terminal
instants, preserving original strings, exact inclusive604800/86400-second floors
and both action-time rechecks; naive/missing/malformed/changed times refuse.
Freeze cap40 with historical overlap exclusions and no refill. Useful preparation,
honest complete empty/no-repo evidence and incomplete enumeration are distinct;
empty leaves retain native inventory/peer/history receipts and emit no request.
Render one supported task/session-bound request with omitted or relative cwd,
actual remaining budget and preserved TMPDIR; submission rejection/uncertainty
ends that attempt without an alternate or retry. The outer host observer requires
readable self context, host-init/unstacked proc and protected/mount metadata,
including workspace .codex/.aws without credential-content reads. Root PID1
namespace denial is diagnostic, without a new equality veto. An incomplete
baseline admits no batch; native batch failure and postcheck failure/drift both
remain nonzero/incomplete with their original causes. Only the default packaged
batch/candidate runner executes; scanner/action gates are unchanged. Local report
or persisted result is distinct from canonical lifecycle/output publication;
failed/unpublished evidence overrides local success. Known-excluded nonempty inventories finish no-action only with verified
same-bound preparation, original/copied inventory and manifest/history hashes,
owner/native receipts and independently consistent excluded rows; a state label
alone is insufficient. Result admission/pending remains separate from evidence
completeness: missing inventory/baseline/postcheck stays blocked/nonzero. Native
batch argv/cwd/exit/raw is persisted before postwrites; after/sequence write
failure retains source/binding and both causes as unknown/nonzero, using bounded
captured cleanup_sequence_fallback JSON if the sequence cannot be persisted.
JSON writers flush, fsync and close a one-attempt .NAME.pending sibling before
publishing the canonical name with an exclusive hard link; existing canonical or
pending evidence is never replaced. Pending siblings remain evidence, are never
consumed as canonical receipts, and need no unlink for failure safety. This does
not add a directory-fsync/crash-durability guarantee. On observer write failure, report
--sequence must select the exact captured cleanup_sequence_fallback JSON file;
report retains its native batch/source/binding/write causes, blocks even against
success-valued pending bytes, and separately retains admitted-result/pending facts.
A missing canonical sequence also produces blocked intent with an evidence error.
The local evidence ordering is attempt envelope → fresh native gates → canonical
eligibility → first mutation → native terminal outcome → verified fsynced journal
append → canonical reference-only acknowledgement → whole-coverage admission.
The batch journal is the sole ordered outcome ledger. Direct Bash use needs the
same bound manifest/history/journal/evidence sink; stdout alone grants no action.
Cache isolation-directory creation is mutation. Failed rename/rmdir causes and
separate original/cache/directory identity and measurements survive; only positive
restoration/absence/source/status/raw-index proof admits safe refusal. Residue or
uncertainty halts. Git read observations suppress optional index refresh.

Each attempt and acknowledgement uses the existing exclusive pending write,
flush/fsync/close and exclusive hard-link publisher. A canonical file with its
identical expected staging sibling is valid; pending alone, conflict or collision
is not. Acknowledgements reference binding, envelope/eligibility identities and
exact journal prefix/row range/hash/ordinal, without a second success ledger.
Batch resume and report preflight the entire bounded namespace and journal before
any skip/admission; orphan or unacknowledged terminal evidence blocks, including
when fallback is missing/truncated. Explicit native failure/interruption wins over
readable files. Same-batch acknowledged prefix progress and later enclosing native
batch/sequence/postcheck completeness are separate prerequisites. No automatic
recovery or directory-fsync/crash-atomic guarantee follows. Historical bytes remain
historical, with missing new-contract proof explicit; never rebind or retry them.

Captured native uncertainty (output cap, timeout, spawn/capture failure, signal or
failed publication) is distinct from a fully captured ordinary nonzero gate
denial. The writer and every eligibility/coverage/resume/report reader retain
the native cause and block action or next admission, even with healthy enclosing
files or missing fallback. Native action/capture children inherit the candidate
cancellation group. The batch stops and reconciles its owned group before
terminal observations; outer capture allows that cleanup before forced kill.
Residual or unproven survival is unknown and unsafe, never clean completion or
permission to retry. Possible mutation remains partial/unknown without reuse.


A genuine bound completion callback is the final session action. This repair grants no cleanup allocation.

The scan runs only as a task-bound host-visible HappyRanch job. The procedure
authenticates a closed-schema, non-truncated receipt binding the current
task/session to the actual job, agent, stored command, interpreter, resolved
cwd, timestamps, terminal result, complete output totals, and scanner coverage;
it has no direct fallback. Complete candidate-specific task and filtered-trigger
paging replaces the former org-wide capped-history veto. PR evidence is also
completely paginated and repeated; open, closed-unmerged, duplicate, changing,
conflicting, or malformed rows refuse.
Durable preservation may be the accepted ref, an owning origin task branch
whose head equals or descends from the candidate, an owning-task merged PR, or
an any-task merged PR whose head independently contains the candidate. An
existing owning branch is authoritative: non-containment or failed containment
evidence refuses without merged-PR fallback. The
any-task route requires complete stable double-read discovery, `MERGED` plus
non-empty `mergedAt`, base equal to the double-read default branch, and a
complete stable compare; discovery alone and other-task unmerged PRs never
count. An owning branch's open or closed-unmerged PR remains a refusal. Merged
integration may not preserve original commit topology. Whole-worktree removal still
requires cleanliness; only a literal root dependency cache may be removed from
an otherwise dirty worktree, after all cache gates, without changing tracked
source bytes or Git status.
The candidate's containing worktree must be registered at its owning primary's
exact `.claude/worktrees/<TASK>` path on `task/<TASK>`. A complete no-follow
`lstat` walk runs before and at action time, refusing nested mounts,
cross-device/foreign-owned entries, protected descendants, unreadable or capped
evidence, and identity drift. Accounting includes the root inode, and success
requires literal candidate absence plus unchanged protected-path identities.

The database-only reclamation selection helper is called only by `run_step`'s
pre-agent hook, never by the scheduler. Supplied claim counts other than initial `0 -> 1` refuse before its
per-read admission callback or SQL; a valid supplied pair still needs a fresh
durable-owner read. Its named per-read admission callback is supplied by the
hook's deadline/read accounting; it does not load this config, create a
claim, or call the consumer itself. Every helper SQL/decode observation fails closed as `None`;
this preserves the bounded hook admission accounting without implying SQL
preemption.

## Forward-only terminal task worktrees

The existing child hook remains a one-task, five-second attempt and honors maker
`worktree-deferred:` evidence. A newly committed structural root (`parent_task_id
IS NULL`, including workflow tasks) reaching `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`
or `superseded` instead reconciles its parent ancestry tree once. At this root
pass an old child deferral is advisory; the result/history/risk bytes remain
unchanged. Revisit and supersession edges protect paths and never enroll other
families as candidates. Only registered assigned-agent canonical
`repos/happyranch/.claude/worktrees/<task-id>` worktrees are considered.

The original winning owner captures the transition under the existing DB lock.
Bounded raw task-audit lookup and start insertion share that lock; the committed
start INTEGER id plus root id/created_at identify the event. Mutable updated_at
or completed_at never create a new event. Original owners hand their captured
root to the shared batch without a redundant notice lookup or fresh cleanup
budget; separate read-only repeats still observe prior identity and perform
zero actions. An unavailable before/after projection or audit anchor names the
unexamined writer/root and bounded audit frontier, closing action authority for
the entire owner batch. A start without finish stays interrupted/unknown, with
no retry. Failed or uncertain admission starts no removal. A terminal commit
without a start remains missing, never rediscovered by historical startup scan.

Shipping tails include ordinary completion/failure/manager supersession,
prelaunch integrity refusal, newly committed startup/recovery/orphan consumption,
legacy/V2 reaper cancellation, task cancel/resolve/revisit, thread dispatch,
portability, and workflow draft/review terminal/cancel. Root work follows history
and teardown outside DB/publisher/workflow locks. The ordinary run-step owner
carries its capture past journal close; accepted recovery carries it to its
current-owner post-drain tail, including zero jobs. The enclosing run-step
worker also carries workflow events past the host callback's tracker clear and
the dispatcher's invocation close/live-claim release. Async workflow cancel
callers defer committed events while awaiting the synchronous owner off-loop;
their post-owner tail awaits the finite batch outside locks, retaining started
work through cancellation. Portability keeps its eligible legacy child hook
after the existing audit/current-owner checks, outside the DB lock and off-loop;
result consumption's disabled child hook remains disabled. Ordinary nonroot task
cancel also awaits its eligible child hook through the cancellation-safe helper:
a started finite cleanup settles before route cancellation propagates. The
periodic reaper keeps lifecycle and workflow queue notification on its owning event loop and
offloads only the child hooks and captured root batch. Nonterminal or lost-CAS
paths create no event. Schema normalization and already-terminal history remain
excluded.

Dirty, local-only, open/closed-unmerged PR, active session/control/PID/job,
unsettled host/recovery, held, successor-use-unresolved, foreign, protected or
unknown evidence retains. Terminality and a cleared tracker are not quiescence.
The release-bundled `workspace-cleanup/scripts/worktree_safety.py` is the shared
Git preservation, recursive snapshot and protected-identity owner for the root
pass and manual runner. Fresh complete owning PR pages are repeated. A live
owning branch must contain exact HEAD; contradictory/failed evidence cannot
fall back to an old default ref or unrelated merged PR. Only proven branch
absence permits fresh default/squash-aware merged-PR containment routes.

Action admission repeats parent/tree/task/successor/job/native ownership,
registration/common-dir, clean exact HEAD, fresh GitHub preservation, recursive
no-follow snapshots and the unchanged packaged same-user scanner. Only exact
readable name+cgroup exceptions apply; other unknown population retains. No
force, branch deletion, cache-only action or task-tmp reclamation follows.
Success requires actual absence, registration removal, preserved branch and
protected identities. Failure after remove invocation is possibly partial
`action_anomaly`, never retained; later mutation halts. Missing finish remains
missing. Task terminal/results/history/reporting survive cleanup failure.

Each owner batch shares 10s work, including 0.5s cumulative DB observations and
at most 50ms lock admission; 1s cumulative report and 1s cumulative owned-group
TERM/KILL/reap. Caps are16 events,256 nodes,depth64,page64,16 candidate attempts,
5s per candidate clipped to remaining work,200000 recursive/pending entries,
1MiB subprocess output and256KiB aggregate receipts. The ordinary-I/O 12s target
is conditional: synchronous SQLite/fsync/kernel I/O cannot be interrupted by a
timer. Overruns are reported truthfully and stop later actions.

Membership/protection closure and receipt capacity precede every action.
Incomplete closure means zero removal, unknown total and an exact FIFO
parent/cursor/depth frontier with known lower bound. Complete membership with
action limits names every remaining candidate. Receipts distinguish no-op,
removed, retained, action anomaly and unexamined; candidate counts partition
without treating absence as reclamation. Complete requires empty frontier and
remainder, closed protection and successful finish publication. No detached
action, implicit continuation, new schedule or historical sweep is introduced.
Manual/daily age/ordinal/peer/history/host-job/cache gates retain their separate
authority. Root release adds no Web/CLI/configuration control. Future source
applies only after separate deployment; historical backlog needs separate
explicit authorization. Source lint is not owner-lifecycle behavioral evidence.

## Org Content APIs

`AgentDef` in `runtime/orchestrator/agent_def.py` represents an agent file: markdown with YAML frontmatter parsed/rendered by `parse_agent_text` and `render_agent_text`.

Fields: `name`, `team`, `role`, `executor`, `description`, `allow_rules`, `repos`, `enrolled_by`, `enrolled_at_task`, `enrolled_at`, `model`, optional `reasoning_effort`, and `system_prompt`. There is no `session_timeout_seconds` field.

Built-ins accept case-preserving fullmatch `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,31}` through shared `model_effort_args` validation; historical catalog values are metadata, not a membership gate. UI uses plain text: blank on explicit Save maps to null/default, without trimming or truncation. Explicit wire/direct empty strings refuse; CLI clear retains its existing meaning. Custom adapters still require approved declared-value membership (at most16 lowercase identifiers); legacy/model-only effort controls remain disabled with saved values visible. Token acceptance proves syntax/transport only, not native model/account support.

With explicit effort, OpenCode requires nonempty provider/model slash components (additional model path segments allowed), no whitespace/control/NUL, at most one # and a nonempty embedded variant byte-equal to effort; it composes one literal `-m provider/model#effort`. Unset preserves the original model transport. Pi recognizes only its existing finite thinking suffix set; arbitrary safe colon suffixes do not become native thinking syntax. Codex keeps one literal quoted config argv element.

Bounded evidence does not verify dynamic manage_agent update/enrollment at runtime (source only), native models/accounts, full task/thread/dream/wake/schedule lifecycles, host/callback or custom contained-wrapper prelaunch. General integration including callback is SKIPPED THR243seq42; former Python units are RETIRED. No ad hoc Product E2E or protected mutation/repetition proof is authorized.

**THR-095 single-source-of-truth:** `executor`, `repos`, and `model` are read
exclusively from ``AgentDef`` (the ``.md`` frontmatter). The workspace
``agent.yaml`` is no longer read or written by any org-agent path. See
`docs/agent-guides/runtime-and-configuration.md#agent-configuration-single-source-of-truth-thr-095`.

Selected effort follows model into existing task normal/followup/resume/recovery, thread fresh/resume/fallback, dream, wake and schedule executor calls/spec builders. `session_start` (including recovery) and `dream_started` JSON add `reasoning_effort` only when set. Thread invocations retain their existing model column and associate optional selection through `thread_reasoning_selection` audit JSON; wakes/schedules use `work_hour_reasoning_selection` / `schedule_reasoning_selection`. Task/thread/dream/wake/schedule selection uses one loaded canonical AgentDef for executor/model/effort. Wake/schedule selection audit `executor` records the effective lowercase registered provider, or the existing Claude fallback for an unregistered provider; `model` and `reasoning_effort` remain the configured overrides from that same definition. Fallback does not clear those overrides or guarantee native acceptance. The same effective provider reaches materialization and executor construction. An atomic save after the read applies to the next selection. These records describe selection, not provider-reported actual reasoning; token usage model reporting is unchanged. No SQL field or lifecycle state is added.

Workflow authority `_agent_projection` appends `reasoning_effort` only when set. Absent-field canonical bytes/digests match the current projection baseline without effort; clear returns to that shape. Set values participate in canonical snapshot digest/publication/admission at the same existing writer intervals. Retained journal snapshots are immutable and never rewritten. Current profile requirement and activation readers address named agent fields and accept the optional field; digest/retained readers authenticate whole bytes. Older readers may ignore a set effort and older whole-definition writers may drop it: absent equality is not all-old-reader compatibility. Use a compatible writer and clear/reconcile before an intentional rollback. Effort adds no snapshot format bump of its own: the inherited roster writer emits schema2, and current version-aware readers retain schema1 and schema2 roster meanings. Historical bytes and meaning are preserved; effort adds no migration/capsule framework. See the [current contract](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-agent-reasoning-effort.md).

`runtime/orchestrator/prompt_loader.py` is the API for reading/writing agent files: `load_agent`, `list_agents`, `list_pending`, `write_pending_agent`, `approve_agent`, `reject_agent`, `load_terminated_agent`, `list_terminated`, `is_terminated`, and `is_name_unavailable`. Routes and orchestrator code should read through this module against the per-org root.

Founder `PUT /agents/{agent_name}/system-prompt` is a strict prompt-only CAS. It validates through the existing parser/scanners before persistence, uses the same-read canonical SHA256 and short teams/canonical critical section, then refreshes only the existing workspace's selected registered adapter instruction pair. An absent workspace stays absent until explicit init. Reconciliation conditionally restores exact original canonical bytes only while its written revision is current; route-level pair replay also requires owned captured pair state. Detected winners/disappearance are preserved, inner pair compensation is not a global same-UID fence, and incomplete compensation is reported. Audit failure may leave committed bytes; best-effort authority publication may leave readiness fenced. The founder `agent_managed` audit retains scope/actor/source `founder` and `{action:'update',name,source:'founder'}`.

`TeamsRegistry` in `runtime/orchestrator/teams.py` is seeded from `teams.yaml` and auto-persists on `add_worker` and `remove_worker`. There is no `DEFAULT_LAYOUT`; an org without `teams.yaml` is empty.

The separate online founder `POST /teams/ensure-default` creates only an absent
empty human-founder Default. `needs_empty_default` rejects conflicting management
before fencing; `ensure_empty_default` saves a validated candidate before updating
the shared registry object. Canonical/cache and membership prevalidation precedes
the durable lease. The async authority writer gate precedes teams_lock; only
canonical save/cache update and bounded audit use the short canonical fence.
Publication occurs through interval exit, then a read-only interval checks owned
readiness and compares the complete current snapshot. Compatible repeat preserves
workers and metadata without save/audit/publication. It reports incomplete authority
rather than repairing it; existing publication/cold recovery retains ownership.
Only absent display metadata is initialized; task routing and explicit pointers
remain unchanged. See the current THR296 spec's separate online contract.

## Agent Lifecycle: Enrollment, Approval, and Termination

- **Enrollment.** `manage-agent enroll` creates a pending agent file under `org/agents/_pending/<name>.md`. A founder (or team manager with an active session) may enroll agents only into their own team.
- **Whole-definition update.** A team-manager `manage-agent update` first reads
  the active `GET /agents` roster and uses the target row's `revision` with
  the exact canonical content from that same read as `expected_revision`. The
  route rejects missing, null, malformed revisions with 422 and stale bases
  with 409; after a conflict, the caller must reread and deliberately reapply
  its intended field change. A later roster revision must never bless an
  already-composed stale update.
- **Freshness and conditional recovery.** Model/repository deltas compose from a fresh definition inside the existing writer gate. Init owns each target lazily through clone/final capture/bootstrap/readiness. Executor preparation, founder create and approval retain one process interval through their final bootstrap and compensation; competing supported saves queue, with stale bases rejected after an unrelated canonical delta. Request cancellation and SSE disconnect retain the gate until every started mutating worker finishes; admitted request writes finish terminal reconciliation before cancellation propagates, even when cancellation repeats. No teams, DB, profile or publication lease spans await/clone/bootstrap/scans. Repository changes, founder create,
  and approval capture the current prompt/provider after their clone work and
  return 404 if that canonical definition disappeared. Executor switching
  rereads after materialization: an executor or model winner returns
  `409 executor_switch_conflict`, while unrelated fresh fields are preserved.
  If update invalidation fails, its exact original bytes are restored only if
  its written revision is still current; a newer or missing definition stays
  in place and its stale workspace is not restored. Termination's early/late
  archive recovery likewise requires an absent active file and the matching
  owned archive. Enrollment, rejection, and termination make their final
  availability/team/quiescence checks at their respective final boundaries.
- **Scope of these checks.** `teams_lock` and no-`await` segments provide only
  process-local event-loop protection. Synchronous unlocked segments remain
  unlocked; this does not provide global workspace-generation fencing or
  serialize external same-UID/multiprocess filesystem writers.
- **Workflow-authority participation.** Supported org-scoped authority writers
  enter the coordinator gate before `teams_lock`, durably fence around each
  synchronous canonical mutation, and publish the next generation only from
  their terminal success/compensation state. Multi-stage update/termination
  retains the process gate across awaited bootstrap; startup migration retains
  it across the batch. The durable lease and SQLite transactions never span
  scanning, clone/network/host-launch/callback work, awaited bootstrap, snapshot
  capture, or other canonical-input scanning. Publication failure leaves new workflow
  admission fail-closed and is recovered at cold `OrgState.load`; it does not
  roll back or falsify the existing route result after the legacy write
  committed. Direct same-UID edits remain outside the cooperative guarantee.
  Every publication is bound to the writer's invocation-owned `prepared`
  journal before the canonical mutation. An independent coordinator may abort
  and supersede only that pre-file state; file reservation and the final pointer
  CAS require the same journal plus `publisher_invocation`, so a stale publisher
  cannot adopt the newer fence. Cold recovery re-captures against the same
  pre-file journal; an incoherent retry performs no durable write and remains
  fenced, while a superseded retry is refused. Eligible-team selector
  initialization during whole-runtime `DaemonState` loading covers daemon
  startup and runtime register/switch before state publication; dynamic org
  attachment owns the same conditional boundary separately. Raw
  `OrgState.load` retains the coordinator's coherent recovery generation and
  is made launch-ready by its containing supported lifecycle. Founder creation of a new-team manager and
  pending bootstrap-manager approval initialize the selector inside the same
  coordinated canonical change as the roster mutation; policy GET/release
  compatibility handlers also participate conditionally. A missing selector
  fences and publishes exactly once, while an authenticated existing selector
  is read-only and does not advance generation. Existing-team worker creation
  advances only its roster generation and adds no selector-history churn. Common task,
  thread, dream, wake, and schedule launch resolution is read-only and refuses
  an uninitialized selector rather than mutating authority during launch.
- **Cutover request/recovery ownership.** `WorkflowCutoverStore` holds the
  existing `Database._lock` plus a short `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for each atomic
  state/event transition and refuses caller-owned transactions. HTTP holds
  existing `org.db_lock`; no profile lease, filesystem scan, host/network work
  or await occurs inside the SQLite reservation. Full canonical layout/version,
  chain cardinality/edges/keys/digests and marker pointer/time/reason are reread
  under ownership; event1 retains its shipped identity/digest. Progressed event
  SHA256 binds canonical UTF8 fields, fixed verifier/Founder request facts,
  actual org and previous digest. Timestamps must be UTC, not monotonic.
  The original foundation DDL/event1 remain unchanged. Complete F, E and G
  are independently constructed release layouts; the legacy authority oracle
  validates layout/history/data, selects that layout's whole generic+workflow
  reference, and hashes all non-null SQL without filtering. Current v2 remains
  observed-only. Existing-org load/enable do not install the draft extension.
  One cold-load call before teams/settings resumes only authentic requests on validated E/G;
  F retains history and the explicit migration remedy without advancing; pure
  reads never advance. Enable verifies real integrity/FKs and absence of
  contradictory work before compatibility_verified and again before enabled.
  Disable commits its admission fence before separate drain commits. F5 closure
  inspection retains actual owners and terminal history; queued cancellation,
  callbacks and uncertain host reconciliation remain U2D/U4/U5 responsibilities.
  Future activation must consume this same SQLite marker under its separately
  accepted profile-to-org lock order; S1 ships only draft schema/validation, explicit operator migration and fresh
  creation. Activation/dispatch/callback writers remain S2 or later.
- **U2A/U2B boundary.** The readiness verifier is intentionally not consumed
  by task, chain, fan-out, activation, or dispatch paths yet. U2B now routes
  supported machine-global executor-profile and adapter writers through
  `ProfileCoordinator`: dependent orgs are pre-fenced, the existing durable
  writer commits, and only a complete profile-and-approved-adapter closure is
  republished. Adapter approval revalidates its selected intended profile
  (including no target) under the existing adapter writer lock before
  approval, idempotent return or binding. A supported submission that changes
  the target causes the existing 409 `profile_consumer_changed` conflict
  without adapter/profile mutation; stale ownership releases before fresh
  selection. Stable known-target contention remains 409
  `profile_coordinator_busy`, and no-target approval creates no profile.
  No new profile lease is acquired under the adapter writer lock.
  Every supported active consumer creation, promotion, executor
  update and termination maintains its distinct requirement inside the same
  canonical fence; pending-only enrollment/rejection has no active membership.
  Dependency publication checks the actual captured active roster, so missing
  rows cannot prove zero requirements. Canonical discovery is outside all
  profile/publication leases and transactions, bracketed by the durable
  authority revision, and revalidated before synchronization/publication.
  Lifecycle source/target leases release before publication capture and awaited
  workspace work, including compensation. Startup settles interrupted operations before returning
  `DaemonState`; direct-connect `planned` rows remain retryable by both the
  commit route and production sweep, with a terminal re-read under the shared
  profile lease before any U1A fence or adapter/profile mutation. Dynamic org
  attachment synchronizes beneath canonically ordered profile leases and joins
  the shared map before releasing them; closure also binds each local profile
  digest to the current global digest. No workflow admission consumer ships
  before its later unit.
  A genuinely empty default org remains attached with no agents and `teams=[]`
  when its initial authority publication is fenced by the missing default reviewer.
  Attachment proves absence of active and pending definitions and canonical/in-memory
  teams outside leases and transactions, brackets that discovery with the durable
  revision, and validates it under profile-then-org ownership. It preserves the
  initial fenced generation and publication journal; `verify_admission_ready()`
  still refuses `authority_pointer_not_ready`. Outstanding dependency or profile
  operation evidence, unfinished canonical writers, and stale captures refuse this
  exception before synchronization. Reviewer policy and snapshot validation do not
  change; subsequent coherent canonical setup uses ordinary publication/recovery.

- **Approval.** `POST /agents/{name}/approve` atomically moves the pending file to `org/agents/<name>.md`; when that promotion makes the registered manager eligible, it initializes the team's selector in the same workflow-authority canonical change. It then bootstraps the workspace under `workspaces/<name>/`. Approved agents appear in `GET /agents` and `GET /agents/enrollments?status=approved`.
- **Termination.** `manage-agent terminate` archives an approved **non-manager worker** on the caller's team. It is refused if the agent is a manager, belongs to another team, or has live work. Live work includes non-terminal tasks assigned to the agent, already-started thread invocations, firing schedules, running work-hours wakes, running dreams, or pending/running jobs attributable to the agent. If the agent is quiescent, the route:
  - archives the active `org/agents/<name>.md` to `org/agents/_terminated/<name>.md`;
  - archives the workspace `workspaces/<name>/` to `workspaces/_terminated/<name>/`;
  - removes the worker from its team;
  - cancels armed schedules, skips pending wakes/dreams, and declines not-yet-started thread invocations with reason `agent_terminated`.
- **Historic records are retained.** Tasks, task results, audit rows, token-usage rows, thread messages/participants, schedules, wakes, dreams, and archived files are never deleted or rewritten. The agent name cannot be re-enrolled while a terminated record exists, so historical identity remains unambiguous.
- **Fail-closed launch.** The orchestrator and thread runner refuse to launch an agent whose active `.md` file is missing or archived. There is no silent fallback to `claude` for an unknown/terminated agent.
- **Enumeration.** `GET /agents` and the default `GET /agents/enrollments` return active agents only. `GET /agents/enrollments?status=terminated` returns archived enrollment metadata.

## Task Status Vocabularies

Agents self-report `status="completed"|"blocked"` via `happyranch report-completion` (the report verb is unchanged — an agent still self-reports "blocked on jobs"). The orchestrator-owned `TaskStatus` on the `tasks` row is distinct, and under THR-037 Change B (Path B) is: `pending`, `in_progress`, `escalated`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, or `superseded`. (`blocked` is fully retired as of Phase 3 — see the Path-B spec.)

`block_kind` is the waiting-reason discriminant for an `in_progress` task — *what it is internally waiting on*: `delegated` (parked delegation bookkeeping) or `blocked_on_job` (waiting on background jobs). `block_kind IS NULL` has no parked reason; that marker alone does not prove a subprocess is running now. A parent waiting on its children/jobs stays `in_progress` (not `blocked`); the await-founder state is the top-level `escalated`.

`superseded` is a terminal state, peer to `completed`/`failed`. An `escalated` / `in_progress(delegated)` task transitions here when a human-authorized continuation (founder `revisit`, or a founder/manager thread-dispatch) names it in lineage: the predecessor is closed (block_kind cleared, audit cites the continuation root task_id) instead of being re-run. The close never re-enqueues the superseded task; it still wakes a delegated parent via the normal parent-wake path, and the delegated close is gated on all children being terminal so no live sibling is abandoned or SIGTERM'd. It joins every terminal predicate (`TERMINAL_STATES`, `_TERMINAL_TASK_STATUSES`, `_TERMINAL_STATUS_TO_EVENT`) and is completion-class for the thread task-followup: a thread-originated task that is superseded emits its `_maybe_post_thread_followup` system message (`task_completed` kind) just like a normal completion. The thread-dispatch supersede is manager-authorized only — a worker self-dispatch naming `resolves` is rejected (`403 thread_supersede_not_authorized`); the predecessor is never auto-closed by an unauthorized dispatch. Query the backlog with `happyranch tasks --status escalated` or `happyranch tasks --status in_progress --block-kind delegated`.

Manager `supersede` decisions are root-only. The completion consumer identifies a
non-root from the claimed task's `parent_task_id` before calling the generic root
supersession writer; it never infers that cause from the writer's `None` result.
A still-current non-root claim atomically becomes `failed`, clears its own
chain/fan-out state, retains the durable note `manager supersede refused:
non-root task <id>`, and uses the existing failed terminal tail plus delegated
parent wake. Fan-out join context therefore lists the child as failed, and a
serial parent clears `active_chain` before its one wake. If cancellation or a
replacement session wins, neither failure nor wake occurs. Unrelated root
refusals remain silent, while an eligible root still creates and enqueues one
successor. A crash after the child commit but before enqueue is recovered by
startup Branch 2 from the terminal-child/delegated-parent state.

## Derived Work-Status Summary (TASK-5522)

`GET /tasks/{task_id}` carries a read-only `work_status` envelope key derived
server-side (`runtime/daemon/work_status.py`) from the task record plus its
existing audit rows — **no schema change, no synthetic audits, no background
monitor**. It exposes only: the current-session start (latest assigned-agent
`session_start` audit), the last heartbeat with an explicit freshness label,
and the timestamp + concise agent-written message of the latest current-
session `progress` receipt. Chain of thought, command stdout, workspace
paths, session ids, and arbitrary audit payloads are never exposed.

State machine (live-task shape = `in_progress` + `block_kind IS NULL`):

| state | meaning |
|---|---|
| `newly_started` | fresh heartbeat; no current-session receipt; session start < 5m old |
| `recent_progress` | fresh heartbeat; latest current-session receipt < 5m old |
| `stale_no_receipt` | fresh heartbeat; no receipt; session start ≥ 5m old |
| `stale_old_receipt` | fresh heartbeat; latest receipt ≥ 5m old |
| `heartbeat_stale` | live shape but heartbeat ≥ 60s old (existing zombie-reaper freshness semantics) |
| `heartbeat_unavailable` | live shape, no heartbeat observed |
| `unavailable` | cannot derive (missing session_start, unassigned, malformed historic data) |
| `not_applicable` | terminal / pending / escalated / in_progress parked-on-block (`reason` discriminates) |

Policies: `STALE_PROGRESS_AFTER_SECONDS = 300` (5-minute display/derivation
policy — it never reaps or acts); heartbeat freshness reuses the existing
60-second semantics (`2 × HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECONDS`). The current-session
lower boundary is the latest assigned-agent `session_start`; a prior
session's `progress` receipts must never satisfy the new session. Labels say
what is observed — a fresh heartbeat is never presented as substantive
progress, and absent/malformed data is surfaced as unavailable, never
fabricated. Both `happyranch details` and the Tasks UI render this summary;
`runtime/skills/bundled/start-task/SKILL.md` §5 makes the corresponding worker
checkpoint policy concrete.

The same `session_start` row now also carries additive Usage v1 lifecycle
fields: runtime `session_id`, actual spawn `invocation_purpose`, `executor`,
and nullable `model`; `workspace` is unchanged. Spawn purpose follows the
decision-loop versus worker-execution mode, never agent role or root/leaf
shape. THR-247 callback-only completion recovery is `unattributed` and remains
durably distinguishable through `task_completion_recoveries.recovery_session_id`.
These payload fields are not projected by `work_status`.

### Post-deploy operational measurement (not a shipping gate)

The per-task states above make **individual** tasks observable; they do not,
by themselves, measure the population metric this change is meant to move.
That metric is the **share of COMPLETED tasks whose wall-clock duration is
strictly greater than 15 minutes**, measured by the read-only per-org SQL
procedure below — never from `progress` audit rows. `progress` receipts /
`work_status` are a diagnostic companion measure only (see "What this
contract does and does not make observable" below).

**Pre-deploy baseline (authoritative).** Immediately before this change
shipped, **277 of 600 completed tasks (46.2%)** exceeded a 15-minute
wall-clock duration. **46.2% — never 55%** — is the baseline this deployment
is measured against. The post-deploy operational/release target is **<20%**
of completed tasks exceeding 15 minutes, evaluated inside the explicitly
defined post-deploy observation window below. That target is an
**operational post-deploy goal only**: it is NOT a PR shipping, approval,
merge, or CI gate, and no CI or merge check enforces it.

**Observation procedure (read-only SQL, per org).** Each org is measured
independently against its own database — the org boundary is the per-org
SQLite file `<runtime>/orgs/<slug>/happyranch.db` (the daemon's
`OrgPaths.db_path`, `runtime/orchestrator/_paths.py`). Orgs are never
pooled: the evaluator must substitute each org's actual storage scope and
timestamps. The window is **half-open** `[window_start, window_end)` and
uses the task **completion time** (`tasks.completed_at`) for membership; the
numerator applies the same bounds and additionally requires a wall-clock
duration **strictly greater than 15 minutes (900 seconds)**. Wall-clock
duration is the difference between the persisted completion and creation
timestamps (`tasks.completed_at` − `tasks.created_at` — both columns are
non-null on every `completed` row and store ISO-8601 UTC text, so
`julianday(...)` arithmetic applies directly; this is the full lifecycle
wall clock from task creation to completion, an upper bound on active work
time). Run the query once per org DB file:

```sql
-- Per-org read-only observation: completed-task wall-clock > 15 min share.
-- Open the org's DB read-only:  sqlite3 "file:<runtime>/orgs/<slug>/happyranch.db?mode=ro"
-- Bind the evaluator's actual values (sqlite3 CLI: .parameter init, then
-- .parameter set :window_start '<utc-iso>'; .parameter set :window_end '<utc-iso>'):
--   :window_start  post-deploy observation window start, inclusive (UTC ISO)
--   :window_end    post-deploy observation window end,   exclusive (UTC ISO)
WITH windowed AS (
  SELECT (julianday(t.completed_at) - julianday(t.created_at)) * 86400 AS dur_seconds
  FROM tasks AS t
  WHERE t.status = 'completed'
    AND t.completed_at >= :window_start
    AND t.completed_at <  :window_end
)
SELECT
  COUNT(*)                                           AS denominator,
  SUM(CASE WHEN dur_seconds > 900 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS numerator,
  CASE
    WHEN COUNT(*) = 0 THEN NULL  -- zero denominator => N/A, never 0%
    ELSE ROUND(
      100.0 * SUM(CASE WHEN dur_seconds > 900 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) / COUNT(*),
      1
    )
  END                                                AS pct_over_15_min
FROM windowed;
```

Procedure notes:

- **Per-org, never pooled.** Re-run the query against each org's own DB file
  and report each org's `denominator` / `numerator` / `pct_over_15_min`
  separately. Do not aggregate orgs into one denominator.
- **Strict inequality.** The numerator counts `dur_seconds > 900`; a task
  whose duration equals exactly 15:00.000 does not count.
- **Half-open window.** Membership is `completed_at >= :window_start` AND
  `completed_at < :window_end`; a task completing exactly at `:window_end`
  belongs to the next window.
- **Zero denominator.** An org/window with no completed tasks yields
  `denominator = 0` and the percentage is **N/A** (the `CASE` yields NULL) —
  never 0%, which would falsely claim the target was met.
- **Read-only.** The query contains no writes; open the DB in read-only mode
  (`?mode=ro`) or run it against a snapshot/copy.

**Post-deploy observation window.** The operator defines a fixed half-open
window at deployment time — for example the 30 days following the deploy
timestamp — and evaluates the same query with `:window_start` = deploy
timestamp and `:window_end` = window end. The pre-deploy baseline 277/600
was measured with the same definition over the pre-deploy completed-task
population.

**What this contract does and does not make observable.** `action=progress`
remains optional and is the only persisted agent-written substantive receipt
in the current contract. When it is absent, the server-derived `work_status`
explicitly reports `newly_started` (session under 5 minutes) or
`stale_no_receipt` ("no substantive update recorded") for a live session —
heartbeat is liveness evidence only and is never substantive work. That
absence classification makes silence observable for operational follow-up
(a live-but-silent task is visibly distinguishable from one with recent
substantive progress). However, a progress-only audit query cannot prove the
implementation moved the >15-minute completion-duration metric: receipts are
optional and the population metric is defined over completed-task wall-clock
durations, not receipts. The primary metric is therefore measured from
completed-task wall-clock durations by the per-org query above;
`progress` / `work_status` may be used only as a diagnostic companion
measure.

## Manager Decision Contract

Each attachment storage_key can be claimed by one task only, including within
the same tree. Each direct, child or then-leg list allows at most five refs;
keys must be unique across the entire initial decision, including siblings and
nested legs. Carrier top-level refs belong to the carrier; its first leg reads
through ancestry without a second claim. Initial fanout preserves both child
and nested attachment fields; later chain advancement keeps its existing rules.

Recognized initial pre-child errors include too_many_attachments,
duplicate_attachment, task_attachment_not_found, attachment_already_claimed,
invalid_attachment_display_name and unsupported_attachment_type. Feedback gives
the code, escaped key when supplied, and remediation. Correct in a genuinely
new authorized session/result; never replay the consumed result. Each
decision-owning task has at most TWO committed attachment-error feedback
opportunities shared across sessions and initial delegate/fanout/parallel.
Committed A consumes a chance even if B/enqueue is lost. The third recognized
error uses the existing root ESCALATED or nonroot FAILED disposition. A valid
correction after two remains eligible under current authority and revision
limits. Other authoring, revise, slice and total-step rules stay unchanged.


The completion route authenticates the active task/session binding and validates
submitted job waits and structured evidence before persisting a report.
`run_step.py` consumes it into a daemon-owned transition; an agent's `blocked`
report is distinct from the task's stored state. `pr_ci_merge.py` owns guarded
merge verdict extraction: a non-null canonical structured verdict is primary;
unusable structured values cannot fall back to prose. Its tests cover null legacy
fallback and contradictory evidence. A submitted `local_ci` object is strictly
validated by `LocalCiEvidence`, but is not an independently verified CI receipt,
and the daemon does not infer that a task pushed a PR from its prose.

Team-manager completion payloads carry two fields:

- `summary`: human-readable prose stored on `task_results.output_summary` and rendered in details, audit logs, and `task_history.md`.
- `decision`: a JSON `NextStep` object stored on `task_results.decision_json` and parsed directly by `Orchestrator._parse_next_step`.

The child-task brief field in a `delegate` decision is `prompt`, not `brief`. Pydantic v2 silently ignores extras, so `"brief"` creates an empty-brief child task.

Wire fields are defined by `CompletionBody` in `runtime/daemon/routes/tasks.py`;
internal decisions by `NextStep` in `runtime/models.py`. The bundled `start-task`
skill explains usage. The two models serve different boundaries; do not copy one
as a second HTTP schema.

### Active team authority policy

`authority_policy_store.py` owns immutable releases, activation history, candidate
pins, and their transactional linkage. Every uniquely registered live team-manager
launch binds the exact rendered policy to the session; the hook consumes that
binding, never a newly selected current activation. Task, fresh/resumed thread,
wake, dream, and schedule entry points independently revalidate the live AgentDef
and exact single `teams.yaml` registration before selector access or binding. The
bound team, not current registry state or caller input, is carried through attempt,
candidate, evaluation, finalization, publication, and spend identities.
`authority.py` validates authenticated manager
self-evaluation for active-policy sessions and applies daemon-owned cancellation,
budget, lineage, protected-boundary, replay, and CAS checks. Semantic evidence is
advisory to those checks. The single-use continuation envelope grants only the
same-root lifecycle. Static-policy compatibility remains separate. Policy routes
provide the founder-authorized release/activation UI; code landing does not
activate a production policy. Authority hook/store/route/envelope tests cover the
negative cases. There is no universal post-session detector for arbitrary
external side effects or role-based pathname enforcement.

#### THR-229 current candidate contract

The open PR878 candidate joins the v2 path end to end. An authenticated v2
